#2151. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2150. Play: Taarabt's Dribble Dash

Few sights in Championship history are as fondly remembered as Adel Taarabt picking up the ball, dropping a shoulder and leaving a defender sitting on the turf.

The Moroccan playmaker had his critics, and his showboating could drive team-mates and managers to distraction. But when it clicked, there was nobody in the second tier quite like him. In 2010-11 it clicked like never before.

Neil Warnock took charge of QPR in March 2010 and over that summer decided to make the 21-year-old the focal point of his side. It proved a masterstroke.

Taarabt captained Rangers to the Championship title and was named the division’s Player of the Year after a season in which he scored 19 goals, helping the club return to the top flight after a 15-year absence.

He also picked up a place in the PFA Championship team of the season, and won QPR’s Goal of the Season for a superb solo effort against Swansea on Boxing Day.

Now it’s your turn to channel that magic. Taarabt’s Dribble Dash puts you in control of the QPR maverick as he runs at the heart of the opposition.

Swipe, tap or use your arrow keys to switch lanes, then weave past cones, lunging defenders and flying slide tackles on your way to goal. Every level ends with the ball rippling the net, and the further you go, the faster and more ruthless the defending becomes.

There are 15 levels to conquer, from a gentle warm-up session all the way to a nerve-shredding title decider. But you only get three lives for the whole run. Collect gold stars along the way for bonus points, and clear levels five and ten to win a life back. Chargers, trackers and sliding challenges will all be waiting for you before you get anywhere near promotion.

Can you guide Taarabt all the way to the title, or will you be scythed down on a cold, wet Tuesday night? Have a go below, then share your score on Facebook or X and challenge your mates to beat it.

And if you survive this one, try Escape from Neil Warnock elsewhere in our games hub.

In 2010-11 Adel Taarabt ran Championship defences ragged as QPR won the title. Now it’s your turn: weave him past cones, lunging defenders and flying slide tackles, and get the ball in the net at the end of each level. There are 15 levels and only three lives. How far can you take him?

#2148. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

#2147. Moises Caicedo And Kendry Paez Give Chelsea World Cup Signal

Ecuador’s late World Cup surge has given Chelsea a useful development signal through Moises Caicedo and Kendry Paez before Xabi Alonso’s first pre-season friendly on 28 July.

Chelsea confirmed that Caicedo and Paez reached the Round of 32 after Ecuador’s dramatic 2-1 win over Germany.

For Alonso, the contrast between the two Chelsea midfielders matters as much as the result.

Caicedo captained Ecuador through the pressure game, with We Ain’t Got No History highlighting the Chelsea midfielder’s leadership role in a win that rescued their tournament.

Paez, meanwhile, sits in a different phase of his rise.

That should not worry Chelsea. FIFA identified Paez as one of the tournament’s wonderkids, but Chelsea’s bigger win is that he can absorb elite-tournament preparation without carrying a defining role too soon.

Read Chelsea has already looked at how Alonso must handle Paez’s development curve, and this knockout place sharpens that argument.

Alonso inherits a midfield loaded with senior names, transfer noise and immediate pressure.

Caicedo gives Chelsea the proven pillar. Paez gives them the controlled upside.

Ecuador’s run now gives Chelsea both at once.

For Chelsea, that is the ideal succession picture: leadership today, patience for tomorrow.

#2146. Bristol City release video statement on Michael Skubala - it's caused a reaction

Bristol City CEO Charlie Boss has confirmed that manager Michael Skubala retains the club's backing, despite their recent poor run of form in the Championship.

After leading Lincoln City to the League One title with a total of 103 points last season, Skubala departed Sincil Bank in May to take over at Bristol City, but his tenure at Ashton Gate has not gone to plan so far.

Skubala was given strong backing in the transfer market this summer after his arrival at Bristol City, and among the 11 new faces, the club spent significant transfer fees on two of League One's top scorers last season, Lorent Tolaj and Dom Ballard.

After Skubala picked up 10 points from his first five league games, the Robins sat as high as fourth in the Championship table in early September, but they have won just two of their 10 matches since then, with their home form proving to be a particular cause for concern.

Bristol City suffered their fourth consecutive home defeat as they were beaten 5-0 by Norwich City on Saturday, with goals from Paris Maghoma, Harry Darling, Andre Brooks and Jovon Makama and Mathias Kvistgaarden's late penalty sealing an emphatic victory for the Canaries and ensuring it would be another miserable afternoon for Skubala's men on their own patch.

As we head into the November international break, the Robins currently sit 17th in the Championship table, six points from the play-off places, and some sections of the fanbase called for Skubala to be sacked after their humiliating defeat to Norwich.

Many Bristol City fans were expecting a managerial change to be made during the international break, but the Robins released a video statement on Wednesday afternoon, in which CEO Boss confirmed that the 44-year-old will be given more time at Ashton Gate.

"The game against Norwich City was a long way short of our standards," Boss said in a video on the club's official X account.

"In the days that have followed, there have been a lot of tough and honest conversations around the building.

"Earlier this week, the board met, and we agreed to give Michael Skubala the opportunity to turn our home form around."

When asked how disappointing Saturday's defeat to Norwich was, Boss replied: "It was hugely disappointing, and a lot of the fans made their feelings known loud and clear at Ashton Gate on Saturday.

"I want them to know that everyone shares their frustrations, from the owners, to the board, to James Ellis, the players.

"Every single person at this club feels that frustration.

"Michael Skubala, more than anybody, knows that we need to improve."

"We wouldn't have made this decision if we didn't believe that, together, we could get through this difficult period," Boss added.

"My job as CEO is to look for improvement in every area, and that begins and ends with first team performances."

Boss then delivered a message to the disgruntled Bristol City fanbase, stating: "I'd like to thank them for all of their support home and away, I know it's hugely appreciated by the players, and I know it's hugely appreciated by Michael."

After many Bristol City supporters turned on Skubala during Saturday's defeat to Norwich, it is fair to say that Boss' update on his future was not well received, with plenty taking to X to express their frustration in response to the club's video.

While Skubala is currently the main target of supporters' anger, some Robins fans also directed their ire towards Boss and Sporting Director James Ellis, claiming that the pair are not qualified enough to make the correct decisions.

Have Bristol City made the right decision to give Michael Skubala more time? Tell us in the comments below!

However, not all Bristol City supporters reacted negatively to the news that Skubala will be given a stay of execution, with some praising the club for their open and honest communication.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2145. Breaking: Man Utd begin formal progress for Michael Carrick replacement

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United are reportedly looking at options beyond Michael Carrick after the club believes he is not the right option for United.

Former Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann is among the names linked with a possible move to Old Trafford if United

Indykaila, a famous insider account on X, reported that Manchester United have started the process of finding their next head coach. Nagelsmann’s representatives are said to be willing to hear what the club has planned before deciding whether the project would suit the German manager.

Exclusive 💣Manchester United begin process to identify next head coach, sources indicate.Club sources have confirmed that Manchester United have started formal progress on appointing a new manager.Representatives of Julian Nagelsmann are open to listening to the club’s…

United have not announced any change in their managerial plans, so the report remains unconfirmed. Carrick is still in charge, but his results at the start of the new season have given the club plenty to think about.

Michael Carrick did enough during his interim spell last season to earn the job permanently. After taking over following Ruben Amorim’s departure in January, he won 11 of his 16 matches and guided United to third place in the Premier League, securing Champions League qualification.

That run convinced the club to hand him a two-year contract in May. The new campaign, though, has been a disappointing story so far. United have picked up just six points from their first six league games, their worst start since 1985/86.

Their latest setback came against Tottenham on Saturday. Bryan Mbeumo put United ahead, and Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card left Spurs with ten men, yet Dominic Solanke scored in stoppage time to make it 1-1.

Dropping points in those circumstances will only add to the pressure on Carrick, who needs a response from his players quickly.

Julian Nagelsmann has previously been linked with the Old Trafford job, and the latest report has brought his name back into the conversation. The 39-year-old’s representatives are reportedly open to hearing United’s plans, but there is no confirmed agreement or evidence that formal negotiations have taken place.

Carrick’s achievements last season bought him the permanent role, but the opening weeks of this campaign have left little room for further slip-ups.

#2144. Chelsea World Cup Knockout Load Gives Xabi Alonso Pre-Season Problem

Chelsea’s World Cup contingent has moved from a group-stage watchlist into a genuine Xabi Alonso planning problem.

Chelsea’s own tournament update confirms that Enzo Fernandez, Mike Penders, Moises Caicedo, Kendry Paez, Malo Gusto and Jorrel Hato have reached the Round of 32.

England have also progressed after their final Group L win over Panama, which keeps Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah inside the wider knockout picture.

For supporters, that is a status marker. For Alonso, it is where summer pride starts to become pre-season compression.

The concern is not simply volume. It is where the minutes are landing.

Enzo has not missed a minute for Argentina, while Caicedo has remained central for Ecuador and captained them in the 2-1 win over Germany.

Those are not fringe workloads. They are the two midfield profiles Chelsea have built large parts of their control game around.

Alonso’s first Chelsea pre-season was always going to demand clarity through the middle of the pitch.

Instead, the players most central to that reset are still operating in knockout football.

Caicedo’s Ecuador role is the cleanest example. Read Chelsea has already assessed how his captaincy display against Germany strengthened his leadership case, but the next layer is more complicated.

Every additional knockout tie gives him more tournament rhythm, more authority and more fatigue.

Argentina’s route against Cape Verde keeps him on a glamorous stage at exactly the moment Chelsea need to settle his role, mood and tactical responsibilities under Alonso.

If Madrid-related noise continues around him, the club cannot afford a sluggish July handover.

Reece James started England’s first two matches before a hamstring issue ruled him out of the Panama game, according to the Guardian.

England still topped the group and moved into a last-32 tie with DR Congo, but James’ situation is exactly the kind of detail Chelsea’s medical team will monitor closely.

Chelsea have already had to plan around a large World Cup group.

The broader issue was clear in Read Chelsea’s earlier pre-season workload piece, but the knockout stage sharpens it because the returns will now be staggered.

Enzo brings a maximum group-stage load. Caicedo brings captaincy responsibility and heavy Ecuador minutes.

James brings knockout qualification mixed with another hamstring-management question.

Gusto and Hato remain tied to France and the Netherlands, while Penders and Paez are still absorbing tournament environments before club reintegration.

That is a spine issue, not a fringe issue.

Alonso needs automatisms in possession, spacing around the double pivot, clearer full-back behaviour and pressing triggers from midfield.

Those details do not bed in properly when key players arrive in drips after a major summer tournament.

Players returning from knockout football can bring competitive sharpness that closed-door friendlies cannot replicate.

Caicedo captaining Ecuador through pressure games matters. Enzo playing high-leverage minutes for Argentina matters.

Gusto and Hato absorbing tournament detail also helps Chelsea’s defensive reset.

But Chelsea cannot treat every tournament minute as clean preparation.

Alonso must decide who returns straight into tactical work, who needs a genuine pause and who can handle August without creating an avoidable injury bill by September.

That is where the James situation feels central.

If Chelsea’s captain carries a World Cup hamstring warning, the club’s right-sided structure becomes more than a selection debate.

It becomes an availability question that touches Gusto, the wider defensive rebuild and the need for control from midfield.

Read Chelsea has already looked at James’ captaincy test under Alonso, and the injury layer now makes that test sharper.

This is the hidden cost of a strong international footprint.

Chelsea want players good enough to survive deep into tournaments.

Alonso now has to make sure that status does not leave his first league weeks short of the very control those players are meant to provide.
